We can structure:\n  - Copy x boundary columns: for each owned lz, y: out[idx(x=0)] = in..., out[idx(x=nx-1)]...\n  - Copy y boundary rows: for each owned lz, x: out[y=0], out[y=ny-1].\n  - Copy full planes for gz==0 or nz-1 (all x,y).\nCould launch a small kernel over a 1D grid where total threads = localCount*ny*2 (x boundaries) + localCount*nx*2 (y boundaries) + maybe planes*plane. But overlapping writes (corners) with same values from same input => fine, no race issue (writing same value, and distinct addresses maybe duplicate). Concurrent writes of same value technically a data race but benign in CUDA; to be safe can avoid overlap by handling x boundary for y in [1,ny-2], y boundary for all x. Corners handled by y boundary rows. Then full z-boundary planes include all. But z boundary planes already copied by full plane, so x/y boundary copies shouldn't include those lz to avoid duplicate. Interior pair x0=2, x1=3; but x=1 is paired with x=0 boundary pair, so x=1 computed via scalar fallback in the boundary pair thread. Acceptable.\n- Boundary scalar fallback for a pair may compute one or two cells with branch. The thread handling pair (0,1) does x=0 copy and x=1 compute; good.\n- y and gz conditions uniform per warp/block (y fixed per warp), so branch divergence only x.\n- But loads in vector path: for cell x0 and x0+1:\n  - center: double2 load at idx (x0 even, aligned if base aligned and row stride nx even).
